as indicated on the following agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A)(3). Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at
the Chairperson's discretion. A copy of the agenda background material provided to the Board, except items to be considered in the
executive session, is available for public inspection at the Navajo County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov

need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office at least forty-eight (48)
hours prior to the meeting, so accommodation can be arranged.
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo County makes a video or voice recording
of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of Supervisors' regular meetings are recorded and may be viewed on Navajo County’s
website and social media pages. If you permit your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County Board of Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and
your child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may exercise your right not to consent by not allowing your child to attend/participate
in the meeting.”

BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:
 
a. Presentation to Snowflake High School Girls Basketball Team for winning
the 3A State Championship:
Chairman Whiting stated that they are excited to recognize the
Snowflake High School Girls Basketball Team for winning the 3A State
Championship, which is an incredible achievement that hasn’t happened
in 28 years.  He indicated that the team showed resilience, teamwork, and
heart throughout the season, and that despite losing many seniors last
year and facing injuries, they never doubted themselves. He stated that
they truly embodied a “next woman up” mentality, and it was inspiring to
watch them grow and succeed together.  He congratulated the team,
noting that they made their school and community proud.
Coach Brogan stated that many may have underestimated this year’s
team, after graduating seven seniors last year, but that a busy summer
with nearly 40 games gave them early confidence and chemistry.  He
indicated that a key moment came early in the season with a big win over
defending the state champion team, Page, which set the tone. He noted
that throughout the season the team faced 11 of the top 12 teams in 3A,
which made them ready by the time of the tournament.  He praised the
players for their energy, effort, and dedication, highlighting their
commitment in practice, academic excellence, and great sportsmanship,
including two and a half years without a technical foul.  He stated that
despite some injuries and setbacks, they finished the season 29-5,
showing resilience and strong team unity throughout.
The two team captains shared that from the start, the team believed in
their potential and worked hard all season, noting that everyone stepped
up when needed, especially during tough moments and injuries. They
indicated that they played for each other, supported one another, and their
unity and effort were key to winning the championship.
Chairman Whiting stated that it was inspiring to see the team play with
heart and determination and that it was a true team effort that showed on
the court. Coach Brogan shared some moments from the championship
game.
Supervisor Benally congratulated the team for coming back strong after
graduating seniors and winning again after 28 years. She indicated that
their determination was clear, and more success is surely ahead.
Supervisor Seymore stated that teamwork was key to the championship,
noting that everyone contributed, whether on the court or from the bench.
He indicated that it’s the unity and support that led to success, and that win
or lose, coming together as a team is what matters most. He
congratulated them on their accomplishment.
Chairman Whiting presented the team with medals in recognition of their
state championship.
 
  
b. Recognition of Snowflake D-3 - 215 lb. Wrestling State Champion Devin   
                                                                    3 of 10 
DRAFT

<!-- page 8 -->
b. Recognition of Snowflake D-3 - 215 lb. Wrestling State Champion Devin
Kinlicheenie:
Chairman Whiting stated that Devin is being recognized for his
outstanding accomplishments in wrestling, noting that he is a three-time
state champion, with a remarkable high school career record of 199 wins
and only 9 losses, 8 of which occurred during his freshman year. He
indicated that wrestling runs in his family, that his brothers were also state
champions, and his father, who is also his coach. He stated that Devin
has shown leadership on his team and continues to set a strong example
for others.
Devin’s Father/Coach reflected on a tough but rewarding season coaching
his youngest son. He indicated that, despite early setbacks, the team
pushed through with hard work and grit. He spoke about Devin’s success
with becoming Snowflake’s first three-time state champion and
All-American, noting that he was driven by his exceptional work ethic and
support from his brothers. He indicated that the future of Snowflake
wrestling looks promising.
Devin shared that his success this season wasn't just about him, that it
was thanks to his teammates, coaches, community, and faith. He
emphasized the team's bond, prayer, and support from Snowflake, giving
credit to those around him for helping him grow and compete at the
highest level.
Supervisor Seymore recognized Devin for his hard work and dedication
to wrestling, noting that it’s one of the toughest sports. He spoke about the
difficulty and skill involved, commending Devin for his outstanding
performance.
Supervisor Benally congratulated Devin on his championship.
 
  
c. Recognition of Winslow Division IV - 144 lb. Wrestling Champion Michael
Romero:
Supervisor Benally stated that Michael, a senior at Winslow High, was
the Division 4 state wrestling champion in the 144-pound class, noting that
he earned four pins on his way to the title, including a final match victory
over the top seed. She indicated that Michael finished his career with 189
wins, 59 in a single season, and now holds two school records.
Michael’s Uncle stated that he didn’t like wrestling at first but grew into it
through hard work and support from teammates and family. He indicated
that Michael became his family's first state champion, placing at state all
four years. He stated that his dedication and training paid off and
expressed pride in his growth and success.
Michael stated Michael shared that his favorite takedown was taking down
Devin Kinlicheenie the first time. He indicated that winning state felt great
in the moment, but he quickly set new goals, that he is aiming to become
a national champion or All-American, with plans to keep working hard in
the future.
Supervisor Seymore praised the spirit of collaboration in wrestling,
highlighting the value of friends sharing their best moves to help each
other improve.
 
  
d. Recognition of Mogollon D-4 - 165 lb. Wrestling Champion Hadley   
                                                                    4 of 10 
DRAFT

<!-- page 9 -->
d. Recognition of Mogollon D-4 - 165 lb. Wrestling Champion Hadley
Porter and 106 lb. Wrestling State Champion Anthony Pimentel: 
Supervisor Seymore stated that Anthony won the 106-pound state
championship for the Mustangs in an overtime match, noting that he
dominated earlier rounds with quick pins and technical falls and credited
his coaches and drill partner for his success. He indicated that last year he
helped his team win a title but fell short individually, which motivated him
this season.  He stated that Hadley finished the season 40-3 and capped
his high school career with a state title after placing in state four times and
coming up short in previous finals. He indicated that with a 199-18 career
record Hadley credited his cousin and drill partner, his daily hard work,
and his faith for finally achieving his long-time goal.
Coach Porter spoke about the importance of strong teammates and drill
partners in shaping great wrestlers, noting that the toughest competition
often happens in the practice room. He praised the dedication and hard
work of the team, especially Anthony and Hadley, while also highlighting
their strong character. He indicated that though the team placed second
this year, their effort, discipline, and values reflect the strength of their
community and prepare them for success beyond wrestling.
Anthony spoke about his tough but rewarding season, crediting his
teammates and coaches for pushing him to improve. He shared that the
hardest battles were in the practice room and joked about struggling to cut
weight due to his love for food. He expressed gratitude for the support that
helped him grow as a wrestler.
Hadley reflected on a successful year, expressing gratitude for the support
from coaches, teammates, and his mom. He acknowledged that his
success wouldn't have been possible without their help and emphasized
that all glory goes to Jesus Christ for guiding him through it.
Supervisor Seymore spoke about the significance of these young men's
achievements, noting that they compete in a highly competitive division
that includes a mix of smaller and larger schools. He stated that despite
coming from smaller schools, they managed to rise to the top in a large
and challenging field, making their victories even more impressive.
 
The Board presented all the wrestlers with medals in recognition of their
championships.

b. Presentation to Pamela Harris on her Retirement from Navajo County
after 5 Years of Service:
Eric Scott recognized Pamela Harris on her retirement from Navajo
County.  He spoke about her kindness, helpfulness, and positive attitude,
noting how impressed he’s been every time he interacted with her.
Janelle Linn stated that Pam joined their team just before COVID hit,
which quickly proved her skill and dedication. She indicated that she
played a crucial role during the pandemic, co-leading a team through
difficult times. She stated that her compassion and commitment were
evident, even working late into the night and early mornings. She indicated
that Pam organizes a community-wide Christmas party, providing gifts for
every child. She stated that Pam was deeply appreciated by her
colleagues and would be greatly missed. 
Pamela Harris thanked Navajo County for everything, noting that this was
a great end to her career.  She shared her appreciation with everyone.
Mr. Scott presented Ms. Harris with a gift in recognition of her retirement.
Supervisor Seymore wished Pam happiness and joy in her retirement. 
He stated that her service and contributions were deeply appreciated.
Supervisor Benally congratulated Pam and thanked her for her service.
Chairman Whiting congratulated and thanked Pam for her service, with
recognition of her community involvement. He wished her the best in her
next phase of life. 
 
  
c. Consideration of a Proclamation proclaiming April 2025, as "National
County Government Month": 
Chelle Ewald presented a proclamation declaring April 2025 as National
County Government Month. She stated that the observance highlights the
important role counties play in ensuring the health, welfare, and safety of
residents. She noted that Alicia, the Communications Director, is leading
efforts to engage the community through digital tours, sharing department
statistics, and promoting civic learning through interactive tools like the
iCivics County Work game. She read the proposed proclamation.
Chairman Whiting shared his appreciation for the presentation and for
Alicia and the team’s efforts in communicating the county’s work.  He
noted that while many citizens interact with the county, they may not fully
understand its role, and these efforts help bridge that gap.
Supervisor Benally made a motion to approve a Proclamation

REGULAR AGENDA:
 
1. Presentation regarding the current wildfire conditions, fire restrictions and
mitigation efforts throughout the region:
A presentation was shown. Catrina Jenkins presented a drought and fire
conditions update, noting that severe and extreme drought persists across
the region, with increased fire risk forecasted in the coming months. She
indicated that Stage 1 fire restrictions remain in place, including a ban on
debris burning and suspended permits. She stated that residents and
visitors are urged to exercise caution, especially regarding vehicle-related
fire risks. She shared her appreciation for support of the recent Wildfire
Preparedness Expo, which had a strong turnout. She stated that the
community is encouraged to attend the upcoming Firewise Block Party on
July 12, 2025.  She shared her appreciation to APS for their significant
support of the Expo.
A presentation was shown.  Janet Dean, with APS, provided an update on
APS's wildfire mitigation efforts in response to increasing drought and fire
risks. She highlighted their comprehensive strategy, including vegetation
management, grid hardening, advanced monitoring with AI-powered fire
detection cameras, and operational changes like public safety power
shutoffs (PSPS). She indicated that the shutoffs are a last-resort safety
tool to proactively cut power during extreme fire conditions. She stated
that APS is also working closely with local partners and communities,
including Navajo County, to prepare and inform customers, emphasizing
early warnings, communication, and public safety.
Supervisor Seymore expressed gratitude to APS for sponsoring the
event, and thanked Catrina, Supervisor Whitesinger, and county staff for
their efforts in organizing it. He indicated that the event was a great
success and much needed in the community.
Supervisor Benally thanked APS for its community outreach and
emphasized the importance of continued education on fire mitigation. She
spoke of the recent brush fires on tribal lands and stressed the need to
keep communities informed. She thanked Katrina for providing timely
emergency information to residents and visitors.
Ms. Dean explained that while only a few power lines in Navajo County
are currently impacted and more will likely be added in the future due to
worsening drought conditions. She emphasized the importance of ongoing
communication to help the public understand the necessity of the
program.  She indicated that unlike California, they do not expect
multi-day outages, estimating most shutoffs to last around 20 hours. 
Chairman Whiting expressed gratitude to APS, Catrina, Vice Chair
Whitesinger, and others involved in wildfire preparedness. He
acknowledged the growing threat of natural disasters and commended
APS for proactively working to mitigate wildfire risks. He shared his
appreciation to APS for their collaborative approach and commitment to
educating and informing partners and the community.
Supervisor Seymore expressed appreciation for the support and help
that Catrina’s husband provides to her, acknowledging his constant
readiness to assist.

The Board returned to Item 2.
 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney
and Board members :
Bryan Layton provided an update on public Listening Sessions held regarding the
development of renewable energy projects in the county. He stated that three
in-person sessions were conducted, along with a side session with local fire chiefs
to address their concerns. He indicated that an online tool for public comments is
still open, and the staff is collecting feedback. He expressed gratitude for the
community's participation and mentioned that the feedback is being compiled into
summaries, noting that they will be reporting back to the board shortly with the
findings and recommendations based on the gathered input.
Supervisor Seymore expressed gratitude for the work done on the Listening
Sessions.  He shared a personal reflection on Easter, emphasized the
significance of Easter as a time to remember the resurrection of Jesus Christ and
the gift of eternal life. He shared a personal story about visiting the Holy Land and
experiencing the empty tomb, reinforcing their belief that Christ lives. He spoke
about having reverence and gratitude during the Easter season and reflected on
the protection received during the Rodeo-Chediski fire. He thanked his fellow
supervisors, staff, and medical professionals for their support, especially after
overcoming a serious health challenge.
Supervisor Benally shared a personal reflection from her time as a critical care
nurse in the open-heart unit, where she cared for a patient who received a heart
transplant over the Easter weekend. She reflected on the connection between the
patient’s story and the sacrifice of Jesus, who died so others could live. She
reported about the ongoing construction work in Kayenta, including road upgrades
to improve safety, and efforts to advocate for funding for tribal road improvements
at both the state and federal levels.
 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC:
There were no public comments.

Title: Individual Tax Exemptions with waiver 
Submitted For: Michael Montandon, Assessor 
Submitted By: Marlene Sample, Chief Deputy Assessor
Department: Assessor
Motion before the Board:
Tax exemptions filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver
Background:
Background: Pursuant to ARS §42-11153. “If a widow, widower or disabled person whose property is
exempt from tax under section §42-1111, the person may have the waiver redeemed by the County Board
of Supervisors at any regular meeting, except that no taxes that were due and payable before the petition
was submitted may be refunded or abated.” The following applications for tax exemption were filed past
the March 1 deadline, therefore, pursuant to ARS 42-11153, a “Request for Redemption of Waiver” has
been signed by each of the following:

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. f.

Title: Agreement for Banking Services with Wells Fargo Bank, N.A.
Submitted For: Danielle Earl, Treasurer 
Submitted By: Kolton Root, Sr. Procurement Officer
Department: Treasurer
Motion before the Board:
Agreement for Banking Services with Wells Fargo Bank, N.A.
Background:
This is a one-year agreement utilizing Graham County's cooperative contract to provide banking services
to Navajo County with the option to renew for (2) one-year terms. Wells Fargo Bank, N.A. has held the
Navajo County banking services contract for the past 10 years.
Graham County and Navajo County are members of the Strategic Alliance for Volume Expenditures
(S.A.V.E.), a cooperative purchasing agreement in Arizona that allows Navajo County to utilize Graham
County's existing contract with Wells Fargo. Utilizing cooperative contracts allows the County to save
time and resources by leveraging pre-negotiated agreements, ensuring competitive pricing and
compliance without the need for a lengthy formal solicitation process.
This contract will take effect on July 1, 2025.

AGREEMENT FOR BANKING SERVICES 
This Agreement for Banking Services (“Agreement”) is made and entered into this 22nd day of April, 
2025 by and between County of Navajo (the “County”), and Wells Fargo Bank, N.A. (“Bank”). 
RECITALS: 
A. On or about June 7, 2023, Bank entered into a Master Banking Services Agreement with Graham
County (“Graham”) to provide banking and treasury management services (“MBSA”), procured
and awarded through a competitive and open procurement process; and
B. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-2632, an Arizona public procurement unit may participate in Graham
County’s Master Banking Services Agreement to the extent further agreed upon in writing by that
public procurement unit and Bank.
C. Graham County and Navajo County are members of the Strategic Alliance for Volume
Expenditures (S.A.V.E.), a cooperative purchasing agreement in Arizona  that allows Navajo
County to utilize Graham County’s existing contract with Wells Fargo. The County represents that
it has the authority to enter into the Agreement with Bank, participating in on the MBSA pursuant
to Arizona law.
AGREEMENT: 
NOW THEREFORE, in consideration for the mutual promises contained herein, the parties agree as 
follows: 
1. Services Purchased: Bank shall provide to the County the banking and treasury management
services, as specified in the MBSA including the exhibits referenced in that section. A copy of
the MBSA (including all Exhibits and Addendum) is attached as Exhibit 1 to the Agreement and
incorporated herein.
2. Payment: Payment to the Bank shall be made in accordance with Section 4 of the MBSA  and
Exhibit 2 of this Agreement (pricing for additional services that was recommended in the MBSA
but for which pricing was not included).
3. Terms and Conditions of MBSA Apply: Except as set forth herein, all provisions of the MBSA
are incorporated in and shall apply to this Agreement as though fully set forth herein, including
that all references to Graham County shall mean County of Navajo.  For the avoidance of doubt,
the Agreement will not be affected by any subsequent amendments to or termination of the
MBSA, which will be applicable solely to the relationship between Graham County and Bank.
Any subsequent amendment or modification to the Agreement must be made in accordance
with the terms hereof and would be subject to any applicable restriction or limitation under
applicable law.
4. Term: This Agreement shall commence on July 1, 2025  (the “Commencement Date”) and shall
continue until June 30, 2026, unless terminated, cancelled, or extended as otherwise provided in
the Agreement. The parties shall have the option to mutually agree in writing to  renew for up to
two (2) additional one (1) year terms. This Agreement shall have no binding effect or force prior
to the Commencement Date, notwithstanding execution of this Agreement by the parties.
5. Conflicts: If there is a conflict between the terms and conditions of Exhibit 1 or Exhibit 2 and the
terms and conditions of this Agreement, those of this Agreement will control.  If there is a conflict
among terms and conditions within the documents that make up Exhibit 1, such conflict will be
resolved as set forth in Exhibit 1.

Disclosures
 We created this proposal for you based on our understanding of your requirements and the services in which you
 expressed interest. This proposal confirms the deposit and treasury management services and the pricing we plan
 to provide you based on certain assumptions including projected volumes and other relevant information
 you provided. 
  
 This document is confidential. Please do not share it without first obtaining our written permission. The services
 and pricing contained in this proposal are valid for 90 days. The pricing is subject to change if the actual volume
 or scope of services differs from the assumptions upon which we based the pricing. 
  
 Please let us know if you believe this proposal does not accurately represent the prices or services we discussed
 with you. If you have questions about the services in this proposal, please contact your Treasury Management
 Sales Consultant or visit:
 Client Analysis Statement QRG: https://wellsoffice.wellsfargo.com/ceopub/assets/pdf/client-analysis-statements/Client_Analysis_Stmnts_QRG.pdf
 Element Glossary - PDF: https://wellsoffice.wellsfargo.com/ceopub/assets/pdf/client-analysis-statements/cas_elem_gloss.pdf
 Element Glossary - Excel: https://wellsoffice.wellsfargo.com/ceopub/assets/mso/PRC/client_analysis_stmt/cas_elem_gloss.xlsx
  
 Regarding your services
  
 Recoupment Fees
 We may assess a Recoupment Fee (Wells Fargo code IAMTH) to partially recover insurance premiums we pay to the FDIC
 for deposit insurance. Recoupment Fee is subject to change by bank at any time. See Commercial Account Agreement
 for complete details.
  
 Fee Calculation:
 Per one thousand dollars of monthly average ledger balance you maintain in your account, multiplied by 0.12750. The FDIC
 does not charge a bank's depositors for deposit insurance or require banks to pass the cost of deposit insurance on
 to their depositors. The FDIC does, however, permit a bank to recoup the cost of deposit insurance from its depositors,
 so long as the fee the bank charges its depositors does not reveal information that could be used to determine the
 bank's confidential supervisory ratings or mislead depositors by implying the FDIC is charging the fee.
  
 Overdraft Fees
 We may assess a $35 Overdraft Fee (Wells Fargo code 24250) for any item we pay into overdraft. We limit our overdraft paid
 fees to four per business day.
  
 Use of Uncollected Funds Fee
 We may assess Use of Uncollected Funds fee (Wells Fargo code ODACT) for each day your account has a daily negative
 collected account balance from uncollected funds being used. Fee Calculation: Average Daily Negative Collected Balance
 multiplied by Negative Collected Use of Funds Rate (Prime +3%) multiplied by Days of Month divided by 360.
  
 Wire Book Transfer
 A Vantage/API Wire book transfer is between two accounts initiated via Vantage/API Wires Book Transfer workflow. A Payment
 Manager Book transfer is charged when the debit and credit accounts are setup in a single Vantage Company ID and
 entitled to Book Transfer.
  
 Services with No Volumes
 Services listed with zero volume are not included in the estimated monthly analysis fee but are included in the event
 the service is used in the future.

 information herein. If you have received this message in error, please advise the sender immediately by reply

Title: FY26 Family Counseling/County Match Request
Submitted For: Jason Cash, Chief Probation Officer 
Submitted By: Laura Mudge, Chief Financial Officer
Department: Probation
Motion before the Board:
Resolution 09-2025 approving the Family Counseling Grant budget for FY26 funding of $11,692.00 and
$2,923.00 in matching funds
Background:
This is a renewable grant that has been established for thirty (30) + years which pays juvenile counseling
for families. If not approved, the County would lose additional funding from the state and would not have
money for family counseling for juveniles.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2026
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 2923
Fiscal Impact:
It is the recommendation that the Navajo County Board of Supervisors submit the County match of
$2,923.00to the Probation Department in order to receive the Family Counseling Grant from the
Administrative Office of the Courts of $11,692.00 for a total of $14,615.00

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. j.        
Meeting Date: 04/22/2025  
Title: Resolution for Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank
Submitted By: Kenichi Maruyama, Strategic Initiatives Manager
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Motion before the Board:
Resolution 10-2025 supporting the Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank to apply for the 2025 Fort
McDowell Yavapai Nation grant opportunity
Background:
The passage of Proposition 202 by the voters of Arizona in November 2002 set the stage for new gaming
compacts between the State and the respective tribes. An important provision of Proposition 202 is the
sharing of gaming revenues with the State, allowing a portion of the shared revenue to be retained and
distributed by a tribe itself.
Fort McDowell Yavapai Nation (FMYN) funds Arizona municipalities for their "programs and projects
that focus on education, public safety, mitigation of impacts of gaming, health, environment, and
economic and community development." Non-municipal public service organizations (such as public
schools, fire districts, etc.), and non-profit organizations that have tax-exempt status from the IRS, are
eligible to apply however must partner with a municipality for application submission and must provide
services that benefit the general public in or around that municipality. According to the FMYN funding
guidelines, a resolution from the city, town, or county governing body is required.
The resolution will serve as the municipality’s intent to accept any awarded funds and to use them in
accordance with the application. Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank plans to request funding
($6,000) from FMYN for renting a larger space to store more food and supplies and meet the
community's increasing needs adequately.   
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2026
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested: 0
Fiscal Impact:
No fiscal impact is anticipated.

RESOLUTION -25
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS TO
ACCEPT GRANT FUNDING FROM THE FORT MCDOWELL YAVAPAI NATION
FOR HEBER-OVERGAARD COMMUNITY FOOD BANK
WHEREAS, Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank seeks to obtain critically needed grant
funding to subsidize the costs of renting a larger space which will help us store an adequate
amount of foods and supplies to meet an increasing demand in our community.
WHEREAS, Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank went through a detailed process of
researching, drafting, compiling, and processing all the necessary materials to complete the grant
being proposed to the Fort McDowell Yavapai Nation, and the Navajo County Board of
Supervisors, acting as the sponsoring entity of the grant, encourages and supports this type of
proactive work; and
WHEREAS, the grant application completed by Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank
fulfills the priority areas identified by the Fort McDowell Yavapai Nation for awarding such
types of grants to 501(c)(3) non-profit organizations, as this grant proposal will serve the needs
of Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank in the State of Arizona; and
WHEREAS, it is the desire of the Board of Supervisors to support and accept the awarded grant
for Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank by the Fort McDowell Yavapai Nation; and
WHEREAS, it is the desire of the Board of Supervisors to offer their appreciation to the Fort
McDowell Yavapai Nation for their generosity in offering these grant opportunities to food banks
located within the State of Arizona; and
WHEREAS, the Fort McDowell Yavapai Nation grant application process requires a resolution
from the Navajo County Board of Supervisors for the grant application to be awarded.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Navajo County Board of Supervisors does
hereby lend its full  support and approval, and accepts the  awarded amount of the grant, as the
sponsoring entity for Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank, by the Fort McDowell Yavapai
Nation for renting a larger space to store more food and supplies and meet the community's
increasing needs adequately. 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on this
 day of  , 2025.

2 
 
Project Summary 
 
The Heber-Overgaard Community Food Bank (HOCFB) is in a small rural unincorporated area in Navajo 
county.  Our pantry is 100% volunteer run, and we have been working out of a small garage distributing 
food twice a month.  Due to lack of storage, HOCFB has leased a larger space which will allow us to store 
food provided by United Food Bank and will allow for nutritious food to be available throughout the 
month.  While we are increasing our fund-raising eﬀorts; we need assistance with the increased costs of 
the larger space 
 
Project Narrative 
The purpose of this project is to improve community health in our small 3000 permanent residents in 
Navajo county.   We have a 27% poverty rate; 19% of adults and 27.3 % of children report food insecurity.  
We have two small grocery stores that are expensive and 2 dollar type stores.  None oﬀer a wide range 
of produce and other healthy food.  Better access to healthy food will improve overall health of these 
individuals. 
We service only 10% of the population today.  We estimate with expanded hours we will be able to 
address closer to the 19% of adults.  Currently, we serve twice/month for 3 hours each time.  “Neighbors 
in need” drive through, meaning some individuals get too much, others, not enough.   
Hunger happens more than twice a month.   
Our new space will allow us to serve multiple days/week and for longer periods of time; allowing more 
families to participate and addressing the high child food insecurity.  Our new model will permit clients 
to come inside and shop for items they need and will use.  Today, local health providers report that many 
patients say they are unable to get the food they need.  More days and more hours will allow us to 
address this. 
HOCFB is a partnering agency of United Food Bank.  A pantry has been at our current location in a small 
garage for 20 years. Our current organization took over 5 years ago as the Covid Pandemic ramped up.  
We have run out of space to operate at the garage we currently serve from. In addition, today all sorting 
and distribution must be done outdoors.  Our new location will allow us to be indoors, a bonus for our 
clients and our volunteers.  Our goal is to be more eﬃcient and eﬀective at serving our community. 
The increase in space comes with a large increase in rental costs.  Today, we pay $100  per month to a 
local church to rent out the small space we have.   The new larger space was one of the few available 
with the needed access for delivery and parking for clients. However, the new space is $1400 per month 
which is a signiﬁcant increase.

<!-- page 44 -->
3 
 
We are working with other community organizations to help assist us and we have commitment 
from Heber-Overgaard Community Resouce Network.  Still, we expect the ﬁrst year to be a challenge 
ﬁnancially as we outfit the space and move to the new model. 
This year we are QCO certiﬁed and have participated with AZGives and the Giving Machine. We will 
continue to expand our fundraising eﬀorts as we grow. 
HOCFB is 100% volunteer organization with no paid staﬀ. We are very fortunate to have dedicated 
volunteers who support us.  Our biggest expense in addition to the food we provide will be the 
increased rent. 
Your consideration is appreciated! 
 
Signatures 
 
 
Budget 
We are asking for $500 per month, which is $6000 per year

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. k.        
Meeting Date: 04/22/2025  
Title: FY25 ACESDV Delta Grant Memorandum Of Understanding
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  Submitted By: Lynda Young, Office Manager
Department: County Attorney
Motion before the Board:
Approval and acceptance of the FY25 ACESDV Delta Grant Memorandum of Understanding
Background:
ACESDV and the Navajo County Sexual Assault Response Team will adhere to the  Domestic Violence
Prevention Enhancement and Leadership Through Alliances (DELTA): Achieving Health Equity through
Addressing Disparities (AHEAD) protocol, attend regular training, cross-share, and meet monthly for
case reviews.  ACESDV will compensate Navajo County for the participation of the Victim Services
Director, Roxanne Pergeson on the grant program.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY25/26
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested: $2,000.00
Fiscal Impact:
ACESDV will compensate Navajo County for the participation of the Victim Services Director, Roxanne
Pergeson.on the grant program.

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
Between the 
Arizona Coalition to End Sexual and Domestic Violence 
and the 
Navajo County Sexual Assault Response Team 
 
WHEREAS, Arizona Coalition to End Sexual and Domestic Violence (ACESDV) and the Navajo County Sexual 
Assault Response Team name have come together to collaborate and to make an application for the 2022 
CDC Domestic Violence Prevention Enhancement and Leadership Through Alliances (DELTA): Achieving 
Health Equity through Addressing Disparities (AHEAD) grant. 
 
WHEREAS, the ACESDV and Navajo County Sexual Assault Response Team listed below have agreed to 
enter into a collaborative agreement in which Arizona Coalition to End Sexual and Domestic Violence is 
the lead agency and Navajo County Sexual Assault Response Team is a member of the Statewide 
Leadership Team; and   
 
WHEREAS, the partners herein desire to enter into a Memorandum of Understanding setting forth the 
services to be provided by the collaborative; and   
 
WHEREAS, the work to be completed by the collaborative through its partners is to be submitted to the 
Centers for Disease Control and Prevention on or before September 30, 2025.   
 
 
I. DESCRIPTION OF PARTNER AGENCIES 
 
The agencies named and described below constitute the partner agencies: 
 
1. The Arizona Coalition to End Sexual and Domestic Violence was formed in 1980 to unite 
concerned citizens and professionals to increase public awareness about the issue of domestic 
violence, enhance the safety of and services for victims of domestic violence, and reduce the 
incidents of domestic violence in Arizona. Forty years later the Coalition is the federally 
designated dual coalition addressing both domestic violence and sexual assault and serves as the 
information clearinghouse and center of expertise for the state for both domestic violence and 
sexual assault. In this role, the Coalition provides training and technical assistance and informs 
and advocates for system changes to benefit survivors. The mission of the Coalition is: To end 
sexual and domestic violence in Arizona by dismantling oppression and promoting equity among 
all people.  
2. The Navajo County Sexual Assault Response Team (SART) is a program developed and managed 
by the Navajo County Attorney’s Office, Victim Services Department.  Originally established in 
2018, develops and maintains our county's Sexual Assault Response Team Protocol and manages 
our coordinated team comprised of representatives of several local agencies that assist and 
respond to sexual assault crimes/victims 11 years of age and older.  Our team consists of 
representatives from law enforcement, victim services, medical providers, a mental health 
counselor, the Department of Child Services, a forensic interview, and a prosecutor.  
Representatives who participate in our SART agree by signing a Memorandum of Understanding 
to adhere to our protocol, attend regular training, cross-share, and meet monthly for case 
reviews.  Our mission is to improve communication and facilitation for a coordinated response to

<!-- page 47 -->
Page 2 of 3 
 
sexual assault within the county, from the first call through post-conviction.  All collaborative 
efforts support the well-being of survivors and aim to bring the responsible person(s) to justice. 
 
 
II. ROLES AND RESPONSIBILITIES  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, it is hereby agreed by and between the partners as follows: 
 
1. The Arizona Coalition to End Sexual and Domestic Violence will: 
a. Fulfill all project reporting requirements as prescribed by the Center for Disease 
Control. 
b. Fulfill financial reporting requirements as prescribed by the Center for Disease 
Control. 
c. Comply with all relative Arizona and Federal standards, policies, and/or laws. 
d. Insure the project’s financial integrity relative to Federal and state law. 
e. Insure the project’s programmatic integrity relative to Federal and state law. 
f. Serve as project lead and provide overall project management. 
g. Assess the project for programmatic effectiveness. 
h. Provide project outreach and awareness, as appropriate, to all relevant audiences. 
i. Fulfill the goals and objectives outlined in ACESDV’s grant proposal. 
j. Lead the work of the State Leadership Team, providing facilitation and group support. 
k. Work collaboratively with the Center for Disease Control and Arizona Department of 
Economic Security, Division of Community Assistance and Development, Domestic 
and Sexual Violence Programs. 
 
2. Navajo County Sexual Assault Response Team will: 
a. Serve as a member of the State Leadership Team, attending monthly meetings. 
b. Participate in the Environmental Scan of Arizona intimate partner violence prevention 
efforts. 
c. Participate in the State Action Plan for intimate partner violence prevention. 
d. Provide recommendations regarding the implementation of prevention interventions 
in Arizona. 
 
III. TIMELINE 
 
Responsibilities under this Memorandum of Understanding would coincide with the grant period, 
anticipated to be 03/01/2022 through 09/30/2025. 
 
IV. COMPENSATION 
 
ACESDV will provide payment of $2000.00 USD to the Navajo County Sexual Assault Response Team as 
compensation for participation in the meetings, travel and work of the State Leadership Team. Half of the 
stipend ($1000.00) will be paid at the beginning of the work and the remaining half when the prevention 
interventions recommendations are made. 
 
V. COMMITMENT TO PARTNERSHIP  
 
1) The collaboration service area includes the state of Arizona.

<!-- page 48 -->
Page 3 of 3 
 
 
2) The partners agree to fulfil the roles and responsibilities as detailed above to ensure project success 
and completion. 
 
3) We, the undersigned have read and agree with this MOU. Further, we have reviewed the proposed 
project and approve it. 
 
 
By ________________________________   Date _____________ 
Jenna Panas

Title: Arizona State Parks Board Off-Highway Vehicle Law Enforcement and Education Program
Grant 
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Resolution 11-2025 approving the acceptance of a grant award from the Arizona State Parks Board for
Navajo County Sheriff's Office (NCSO) OHV Law Enforcement and Education Program (ASPT #552505)
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office is requesting approval to proceed with the acceptance of an OHV
Law Enforcement grant from the Arizona State Parks Board. This funding will provide for $250,000.00 in
equipment and staffing for OHV law enforcement patrols and education in the State Trust Lands and
USFS lands and roads within Navajo, Apache, and Coconino Counties. The grant requires a 10% match
which can be met in existing equipment use and staff time, causing no impact on the NCSO budget. Once
the resolution is passed, Navajo County Sheriff's Office will be provided with the grant award agreement
which will be presented to the Board of Supervisors.

WHEREAS, The Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) Recreation Fund is gene rated from OHV Decal 
sales and gas tax revenues. These funds provide opportunities for OHV  education, outreach, trail 
building, trail maintenance, law enforcement, signage, support facility development, as well as 
emergency repairs and vandalism prevention; and

roads within Navajo, Apache, and Coconino Counties; and

limited to applications, agreements, amendments, billing statements, and so which may be 
necessary for the completion of the grant funded project.

Title: Deed to State
Submitted For: Danielle Earl, Treasurer  Submitted By: Danielle Earl, Treasurer
Department: Treasurer
Motion before the Board:
Application for Treasurer's Deed for the Board of Supervisors on behalf of the State
Background:
APPROVAL BY THE BOARD PER ARS 42-18261, wherein it reads: If a lien is not redeemed at any
time beginning five years after the lien is assigned to this state, the county board of supervisors, acting on
behalf of this state, may apply for and receive a treasurer's deed to the property under this article.
Title searches must be performed on each parcel and each owner must be served with a certified notice. 
If redemption is not made by law before August 15, 2025, the parcels will then be deeded to the State of
Arizona and turned over to the Board of Supervisors for auction.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2024-2025
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 27,000.00
Fiscal Impact:
Lawyers Title came in with the lowest bid amount of $115.00 per tax parcel number, which includes an
individual limited title report for each tax parcel.  Approximately $27,000.00 to be used for limited title
reports conducted by Lawyers Title.

Title: NEOGOV SparkHire Contract

Motion before the Board:
Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: NEOGOV Spark Hire
Contract
Background:
SparkHire Video Interviewing is a recorded video interview service which will integrate with our existing
NEOGOV recruitment processes.  It allows you to review applications and select which candidates you
would like to interview, but instead of coordinating the schedules of your interview panel and scheduling
the interview, you have the option for SparkHire to send them a link for the candidate to record their
interview at their convenience.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. p.        
Meeting Date: 04/22/2025  
Title: Authorization for Staff to Request FY2026 Congressionally Directed Spending funds for
Fairgrounds
Submitted By: Leah Thomas, Deputy Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Resolution 12-2025 authorizing staff to request FY2026 Congressionally Directed Spending funds for
Navajo County Fairgrounds Facility Renovation
Background:
The offices of Senators Kelly and Gallego are accepting requests from Arizona stakeholders for items to
be included in the fiscal year 2026 appropriations bills. The offices will only consider congressionally
directed spending (CDS) requests that meet specific criteria and priorities, including all funds requested
must exclusively benefit entities within the state of Arizona. Requests sponsored by local, state, and tribal
government are highly considered by the Appropriations Committee.
The Navajo County Fairgrounds serves as a central hub for community gatherings, particularly during the
annual Navajo County Fair & Rodeo. It's a significant location that brings together over 25,000 residents
and visitors for a week of entertainment, including rodeos, carnival rides, and competitions. Navajo
County Fair, Inc. strives to advance the agricultural, homemaking, educational, cultural, esthetic and
scientific interests of the residents of Navajo County. The Fairgrounds is also one of the facilities that can
accommodate large animals for emergency evacuation. 
The Navajo County Fairgrounds has a rich history, and Navajo County has diligently maintained this
county-owned facility with limited resources and kept it safe for county residents and visitors. The
facility will need major structural renovation projects, including replacement of the grandstands, to
continue serving the needs and interests of county residents, visitors, and community stakeholders. CDS’s
Economic Development Initiatives (EDI) account can fund construction, property acquisition, blight
removal or remediation, or other rehabilitation or improvement to public facilities. There is no cost share
required to receive funding from the EDI account. 
In partnerships with community stakeholders, including the Navajo County Fair, Inc. and local
municipalities, Navajo County plans to request $3 million from FY2026 CDS funds for conducting the
much needed facility renovation, increasing the Fairgrounds’ safety and longevity, and ensuring
compliance with fire codes.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only
RESOLUTION ____-25
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
AUTHORIZING STAFF TO REQUEST FY2026 CONGRESSIONALLY DIRECTED
SPENDING FUNDS FOR NAVAJO COUNTY FAIRGROUNDS FACILITY
RENOVATION
WHEREAS, the offices of Senators Kelly and Gallego are accepting requests from Arizona
stakeholders for items to be included in the fiscal year 2026 appropriations bills. The offices will
only consider congressionally directed spending requests that meet specific criteria and priorities,
including all funds requested must exclusively benefit entities within the state of Arizona; and
WHEREAS, requests sponsored by local, state, and tribal government are highly considered by the
Appropriations Committee. Nonprofit organizations must partner with their local government to
apply for this funding opportunity. For-profit organizations are not eligible; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Fairgrounds serves as a central hub for community gatherings,
particularly during the annual Navajo County Fair & Rodeo. It's a significant location that brings
together over 25,000 residents and visitors for a week of entertainment, including rodeos, carnival
rides, and competitions; and
WHEREAS, Navajo County Fair, Inc. strives to advance the agricultural, homemaking,
educational, cultural, esthetic and scientific interests of the residents of Navajo County. The Fair,
Inc. promotes the competitive exhibition of farm products, livestock, home economics, and other
materials in our community; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Fairgrounds has been utilized as an evacuation site for numerous
emergencies in our community. It offers logistical support for the National Guard. It is one of the
facilities that can accommodate large animals for emergency evacuation; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Fairgrounds is one of the county-owned facilities. Navajo County
has diligently maintained the fairground with limited resources and kept it safe for county residents
and visitors; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Fairgrounds has a rich history. The first Navajo County Fair &
Rodeo was held in 1931. The fairground facility will need major structural renovation projects,
including replacement of the grandstands, to continue serving the needs and interests of county
residents, visitors, and community stakeholders; and

<!-- page 78 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF
SUPERVISORS AS FOLLOWS:
 The Board supports staff to request no more than $3,000,000 from FY2026 Congressionally
Directed Spending for conducting much needed facility renovation at Navajo County
Fairgrounds and increasing the safety of county residents and visitors at the fairground.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. a.        
Meeting Date: 04/22/2025  
Title: Proclamation for National Police Week
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Presented By: Sheriff David Clouse
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of a proclamation declaring the week of May 11th - 17th, 2025, as National
Police Week in Navajo County, Arizona.
Background:
In the year 1965, President Kennedy proclaimed May 15th as National Peace Officer Memorial Day, and
the calendar week in which it falls, National Police Week. The members of the law enforcement agency
of the Navajo County Sheriff's Office play an important role in safeguarding the rights and freedoms of
the citizens of our community.  It is important that all citizens know and understand the problems, duties,
and responsibilities of their Sheriff's Office.  It is also important that the members of the Office recognize
their duty to serve the people by safeguarding life and property, protecting them against violence and/or
disorder, protecting the innocent against deception, and the weak against oppression or intimidation. 
Now, therefore, be it proclaimed. 
Recommendation: 
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office recommends the designation and adoption of the week of May 11th -
17th, 2025 as National Police Week in Navajo County, Arizona.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. b.        
Meeting Date: 04/22/2025  
Title: Proclamation for National Correctional Officers Week
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Presented By: Sheriff David Clouse
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of a proclamation declaring the week of May 4th - 10th, 2025, as National
Correctional Officers Week in Navajo County, Arizona, in honor of the men and women whose diligence
and professionalism keep our county and citizens safe.
Background:
National Correctional Officers Week was first proclaimed on May 5th, 1984, by President Ronald
Reagan to recognize the contributions of Correctional Officers to our Nation. Correctional Officers
represent a crucial component of the criminal justice system, play a vital role in protecting the rights of
citizens to be safeguarded from criminal activity, are responsible for the custody, welfare, and dignity of
the people charged with their care, and deserve our highest praise, respect, and appreciation.
Recommendation: 
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office recommends the designation and adoption of the week of May 4th -
10th, 2025, as National Correctional Officers Week in Navajo County, Arizona.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. a.        
Meeting Date: 04/22/2025  
Title: Starlight Pavement Reconstruction Contract Number B25-02-005
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Kolton Root, Sr. Procurement Officer
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the award of Contract Number B25-02-005 for Starlight Pavement Reconstruction to
PAP, LLC.
Background:
This is a pavement reconstruction project located in the Starlight Ridge Estates subdivision in District-V
of the Navajo County. The project includes portions of Rim Road, Wagon Wheel Lane, Taurus Drive,
and Orion Drive. The work consists of removing existing pavement and subgrade, and installing new
geogrid, aggregate base course, and 3 inches of asphalt concrete pavement and other related work.
Sealed bids were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental Center,
Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 PM M.S.T., March 26, 2025, in strict accordance with the specifications on
file. No bids were accepted after 3:00 PM M.S.T. The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15
PM. Four bids were received and PAP, LLC was the lowest responsible, responsive bidder.
Recommendation: 
Staff is recommending award of Contract B25-02-005 Starlight Pavement Reconstruction to PAP, LLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY25/26
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 1189808.00
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted in fiscal year 2025/2026 under the Capital Improvement Projects.

NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA  
CONTRACT 
STARLIGHT PAVEMENT RECONSTRUCTION, #B25-02-005 
 
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of April 22, 2025 (the “Effective Date”) by and between 
Navajo County (“County”) and PAP, LLC (“Contractor”). 
 
1. Scope of Work:  
Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and labor required for performing all 
work for Starlight Pavement Reconstruction, #B25-02-005, in accordance with the 
Contract Documents, in a workmanlike manner to the satisfaction of County through its 
Engineers. 
2. Contract Documents:  
The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the attached ☒ Notice of Invitation for 
Bids, ☐ Specifications, ☒ General Provisions, ☐ Special Provisions, ☒Proposal dated March 25, 
2025, ☒ Bid Schedule, ☒ Surety (Bid) Bond, ☒ Statutory Performance Bond, ☒ Affidavit by 
Contractor Certifying That There Was No Collusion in Bidding for Contract, ☒ Intentions 
Concerning Subcontracting, ☒ Contractors Qualifications, ☒ Plans, and addenda thereto, if any.  
If there is any conflict in the documents, this Contract shall control over all others; the 
Specifications shall control over the General Provisions and Special Provisions; and the Special 
Provisions shall control over the General Provisions. 
3. No Liens / Time for Completion:  
Contractor shall furnish all materials, equipment and labor in accordance with the Contract 
Documents free and clear of all claims, liens and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, 
stated in the Proposal. 
4. Cancellation:  
This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-511 (concerning conflicts of 
interest). 
5. Payments:   
Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the awarded items in the Bidding 
Schedule for Contract #B25-02-005.  Contractor shall submit invoices to County for all work 
performed hereunder.  No later than 14 days after each invoice has been verified and approved 
by the Engineer, County shall pay to Contractor ninety (90%) of the value of said work in place, 
as approved by the Engineer.  The balance of ten percent (10%) shall be retained by the Owner 
until the time of final payment and acceptance of said work, as per A.R.S. §34-221.  The 
provisions of A.R.S. §34-221 are incorporated herein by this reference and shall control in 
regard to all matters of payment.

6. Certifications: 
a. Prohibition of Boycott of Israel:  Contractor certifies that neither it nor its 
subcontractors are currently or will be engaged in, for the duration of this Contract engage

b. Written Certification Pursuant to A.R.S. §35-394:  Pursuant to A.R.S. § 35-394, 
Contractor certifies that it is not currently using, and agrees for the duration of this 
Agreement to not use (1) the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of 
China; (2) any goods or services produced by the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the 
People’s Republic of China; and (3) any contractors, subcontractors, or suppliers that use the 
forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China.  The Subrecipient further 
acknowledges and agrees that (1) if the Subrecipient becomes aware during the term of this 
Agreement that it is not in compliance with this certification that the Subrecipient will notify 
the County within five (5) business days after becoming aware of the noncompliance; and (2) 
if the Subrecipient does not provide the County with a written certification that the 
Subrecipient has remedied the noncompliance within one hundred eighty (180) after giving 
notice thereof, the same shall constitute a material breach of this Agreement, which shall 
then terminate automatically, except that if the Agreement termination date occurs before the 
end of the remedy period, this Agreement terminates on the Agreement’s termination date.  
The County retains the legal right to inspect the records of the Contractor and all 
Subcontractors to ensure compliance with this certification for the duration of this 
Agreement.

15.THE ENGINEER IS NOT RESPONSIBLE FOR CONSTRUCTIONMEANS, METHODS, TECHNIQUES, SEQUENCES ORPROCEDURES OR SAFETY PRECAUTIONS OR PROGRAMSUTILIZED IN CONNECTION WITH THE WORK AND THEY ARENOT RESPONSIBLE FOR THE CONTRACTORS FAILURE TOCARRY OUT THE WORK IN ACCORDANCE WITH THECONTRACT DOCUMENTS.16.ANY WORK AND MATERIAL NOT IN CONFORMANCE WITH THEPLANS AND SPECIFICATIONS IS SUBJECT TO REMOVAL ANDREPLACEMENT AT THE CONTRACTOR'S EXPENSE.17.THE CONTRACTOR SHALL KEEP SUITABLE EQUIPMENTON-HAND AT THE JOB SITE FOR MAINTAINING DUST CONTROLAND SHALL CONTROL DUST AS DIRECTED BY THE OWNERSREPRESENTATIVE.18.NO JOB WILL BE CONSIDERED COMPLETE UNTIL ALL CLEANUPIS COMPLETED, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, CURBS ANDPAVEMENTS SWEPT CLEAN OF ALL DIRT AND DEBRIS.19.SHOULD THE CONTRACTOR ENCOUNTER, DURING HISOPERATIONS, ANY ARCHEOLOGICAL OF HISTORICALFINDINGS, HE SHALL IMMEDIATELY CEASE OPERATIONS INTHE LOCATION AND NOTIFY NAVAJO COUNTY.20.ANY REVISIONS TO THE APPROVED PLANS SHALL BESUBMITTED TO THE COUNTY ENGINEER FOR REVIEW ANDACCEPTANCE PRIOR TO CONSTRUCTION.21.IT IS THE RESPONSIBILITY OF THE CONTRACTOR TO NOTIFYTHE ENGINEER OF ANY DISCREPANCIES, CONFLICTS, ETC.FOUND WITHIN THIS DRAWING SET, APPLICABLESPECIFICATIONS, AND DETAILS.22.ANY INSPECTION BY THE COUNTY SHALL NOT IN ANY WAYRELIEVE THE CONTRACTOR FROM OBLIGATIONS TO PERFORMTHE WORK IN STRICT COMPLIANCE WITH THE APPLICABLECODES AND AGENCY REQUIREMENTS.23.THE CONTRACTOR SHALL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR SUBGRADEAND ABC COMPACTION TESTING AS WELL AS AC DENSITYTESTING IN ACCORDANCE WITH ADOT TESTING PROCEDURES.24.THE CONTRACTOR SHALL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR PROVIDINGCONSTRUCTION WATER.25.THE CONTRACTOR SHALL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR PROVIDINGCONSTRUCTION STAKING FOR THE PROJECT.26.NAVAJO COUNTY SHALL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR MATERIALACCEPTANCE.27.THE CONTRACTOR SHALL NOT STOCK PILE ANY NEWROADWAY MATERIAL ON THE JOBSITE.28.MAILBOXES ARE TO BE PROTECTED-IN-PLACE. IF PROTECTINGIN PLACE IS NOT POSSIBLE, THE MAILBOX SHALL BE REMOVEDAND RESET 2' FROM THE EDGE OF THE NEW AC PAVEMENT.

1.ALL SIGNS SHALL BE IN COMPLIANCE WITH THE MANUAL ONUNIFORM TRAFFIC CONTROL DEVICE (MUTCD), THE ADOTSIGNING AND MARKING STANDARD DRAWINGS, AND THE TRAFFICENGINEERING MANUAL OF APPROVED SINGS.2.THE SIGN LOCATIONS AND THE POST LENGTHS AREAPPROXIMATE. THE CONTRACTOR SHALL VERIFY THE SIGNLOCATION AND ACTUAL POST LENGTHS WITH THE ENGINEERPRIOR TO INSTALLING SIGNS.3.THE BOTTOM OF EACH SIGN INSTALLED ON A NEW POST SHALLBE AT LEAST 7 FEET ABOVE THE NEAREST EDGE OF PAVEMENTAND AT LEAST 7 FEET ABOVE THE GROUND UNDER THE SIGN.4.FOR SIGNS INSTALLED ON NEW POSTS, THE CONTRACTOR SHALLINSTALL THE NEW SIGNS SO THE NEAREST EDGE OR CORNER OFEACH SIGN IS OFFSET 12 FEET FROM THE NEAREST EDGE OF THEPAVEMENT, EXCEPT AS OTHERWISE INDICATED.
5.ALL SIGNS SHALL BE FABRICATED OF FLAT SHEET ALUMINUM OREXTRUDED ALUMINUM AS INDICATED IN SECTION 608.6.THE RETROREFLECTIVE SHEETING ON ALL NEW SIGNS SHALLMEET THE CRITERIA ESTABLISHED FOR TYPE IX OR XI SHEETINGIN ACCORDANCE WITH ASTM D4956. ALL NEW YELLOW ANDYELLOW-GREEN SIGNS SHALL HAVE FLUORESCENT SHEETING.7.ALL NEW GROUND-MOUNTED SIGNS SHALL BE INSTALLED ONNEW SQUARE TUBE POSTS WITH FOUNDATIONS IN ACCORDANCEWITH STD DWG S-3 WITH TWO NUTS PER BOLT OR ON NEWBREAKAWAY POSTS IN ACCORDANCE WITH STD DWG S-2, S-4, S-5.AND S-6.8.WHERE INDICATED IN THE SIGN SUMMARY, NEW SLIP BASESSHALL BE INSTALLED IN ACCORDANCE WITH STD DWG S-3.9.WHERE INDICATED IN THE SIGN SUMMARY, THE CONTRACTORSHALL INSTALL HORIZONTAL STRINGERS IN ACCORDANCE WITHSTD DWG S-3.
10.ALL BOLTS USED TO INSTALL SIGNING SHALL HAVE HEX HEADS,NOT SLOTTED HEADS, AND SHALL NOT BE PAINTED.11.THE CONTRACTOR SHALL USE ONLY ZINC PLATED STEELWASHERS, NOT NYLON WASHERS, BETWEEN EACH BOLT HEADAND THE FACE OF THE SIGN PANEL. THE WASHERS SHALL NOTBE PAINTED.12.SHOP DRAWINGS WILL BE REQUIRED.13.THE ENGINEER MAY MODIFY THE SIGNING PLANS.

Board of Supervisors Regular 3. b.        
Meeting Date: 04/22/2025  
Title: Attachment D to Navajo Nation IGA for N8065 roadway maintenance materials
Submitted By: Leah Thomas, Deputy Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Nicole Reske
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Attachment D to The Navajo Nation Intergovernmental Agreement for assistance with
roadway maintenance materials for the N8065 Road Maintenance Project.
Background:
Forest Lake Chapter, in coordination with the Navajo Division of Transportation, has requested assistance
for roadway maintenance materials for the N8065 Road Maintenance Project. N8065 is an unpaved
roadway which provides access to the Chapter House, businesses & residents, school and other road
users. The scope of work is for aggregate base gravel placement on a segment of N8065 starting at
-110.240869W, 36.221569N and ending at -110.227568W, 36.229547N for a total of 0.8 miles. District 1
Special Road Funds/Navajo Nation Funds will be used to purchase the materials for this project.
 
Recommendation: 
Approval 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 25-26
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested: 99,952.00
Fiscal Impact:
Attachments
Attachment D 
N8065 Scope of Work, Quote(s), Map

<!-- page 111 -->
ATTACHMENT D
To the  Intergovernmental  Agreement Between
THE NAVAJO  NATION
AND
NAVAJO COUNTY
The ATTACHMENT D is approved pursuant to the Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) between
the Navajo  Nation and  Navajo  County, effective date of April 3rd, 2024. The IGA was assigned
Contract No . MOU279 .  This ATTACHMENT  D is hereby  approved  by the signatures
below in  accordance  with Paragraph 1 .1 Initial and  Additional PROJECTS of the IGA between the
Navajo  Nation  and Navajo  County  (Contract  No.   MOU279  ) and provides for the following :
SCOPE OF WORK:
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board's regular meeting on
April 22, 2025, has approved the expenditure of up to $99,952.00  in District I Special Project Road
Funds to be used for the purchase of Type 2 ABC Gravel Material for N8065 Road Maintenance
Project. Such material shall be used for road maintenance activities wh ich are located on the Navajo
Reservation, Forest Lake Chapter, in Navajo County, within Supervisorial District 1.
For any material acquired in whole or  in part with the Allocated Funds, Navajo County
(County) shall have no responsibility  or liability whatsoever in regard to such material. The
Nation shall be responsible for identifying , prioritizing , scheduling, and performing all
maintenance  activities for which the Allocated Funds are used in whole or in part. It is
understood that the County's sole obligation hereunder is to make payment of the Allocated
Funds.
This Attachment shall be valid effective April 22 nd, 2025, through  June 30 , 2026. Any Allocated
Funds  that are unused  as of June 30, 2026, shall no longer  be available for distribution
pursuant to this ATTACHMENT D.
The material  purchased by  the County with  the allocated funds shall be delivered to the project
site in Forest Lake  at a time and location to be determined :
The request for funds will be sent to:
Jeanine Carruthers
Navajo County Public Works Department
P.O. Box 668
Holbrook, AZ 86025

• Material  acquisition  details:

• Date(s),  Type (s) and quantity  of material  delivered
• Date(s), Location(s)  and Road Maintenance  Activitie s performed  with Material  purchase.
The NATION  certifies  that the road(s) which are the subject of the PROJECT  described in
Attachment D to the IGA are open  to the public , were laid out, constructed and  opened before
June 13, 1990, and are, to the best of the NATION 's knowled ge, information and belief, eligible
for expenditures from  the Arizona Highway User Revenue Fund under the applicable Arizona
statutes and constitutional provisions.
The NATION agrees to cooperate fully in any audit of the use of HURF monies on the
PROJECT as may be conducted by or under the direction of the Arizona Auditor General.
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the  duly authorized  representatives  of the parties have executed this
Attachment D on the dates affixed by their signature s.
